These facts include:\r\n\r\n- module and directory maps\r\n- shell and platform details\r\n- test and lint commands\r\n- virtual-environment requirements\r\n- handover-file paths\r\n- repository-specific gotchas\r\n- safety limits\r\n\r\nBefore starting real work, read `repo_overview.md` if it exists.\r\n\r\nIf this file conflicts with the code or `repo_overview.md`, follow the code.\r\nRecord the discrepancy in `TODO.md`.\r\n\r\n## Writing and Output Rules\r\n\r\nApply these rules to prose, summaries, handovers, and instructions.\r\n\r\nProtocol literals and structured state take precedence over stylistic rules.\r\n\r\n- Use short, active sentences. Aim for 20 words or fewer.\r\n- Name the actor and object.\r\n- Give one action per sentence and per numbered procedure step.\r\n- State conditions and prerequisites before the actions they control.\r\n- Use one consistent term for each entity.\r\n- Define necessary uncommon terms on first use.\r\n- Avoid passive voice, vague references, filler, needless synonyms, and unexplained jargon.\r\n- Preserve code, identifiers, paths, commands, API names, literals, values, and quoted errors exactly.\r\n- Do not invent technical names or state. Report uncertainty explicitly.\r\n- Keep one topic per paragraph.\r\n- Explain general rules before specifics, unless reporting an observed issue.\r\n- Use numbered lists for procedures and bullets for non-sequential facts.\r\n- Separate facts, assumptions, actions, and results when mixing them could cause ambiguity.\r\n\r\n## Editing This File\r\n\r\nDo not edit this file directly.\r\n\r\nFor a pending change, edit a copy. The maintainer replaces this file.\r\n\r\nThis file is the single source of the shared protocol.\r\nOpenCode auto-loads it into every primary and subagent session.\r\n\r\nRole prompts must reference these sections.\r\nRole prompts must not restate them.\r\n\r\n## Role and Interaction Model\r\n\r\nEach role prompt adds role-specific behavior.\r\nIt does not replace this protocol.\r\n\r\nRead each applicable section once.\r\nReference the section instead of repeating it.\r\n\r\n| Role | Responsibilities | Produces | Consumed by |\r\n|---|---|---|---|\r\n| planner | Plan, delegate, verify, own the goal and plan state | Task spec, NAP, closing summary, action line | Worker, explorer, next planner |\r\n| worker | Implement one task, edit, verify, and reach green state | Green commit and worker summary | Planner |\r\n| explorer | Audit, map, and classify gaps | Prioritized `TODO.md` entries and worker summary | Planner |\r\n\r\n## Authority and Recovery\r\n\r\nUse this precedence order:\r\n\r\n1. The current maintainer instruction.\r\n2. Repository code and enforced tool permissions.\r\n3. `AGENTS.md`.\r\n4. The active role prompt.\r\n5. Distilled role memory.\r\n6. General model knowledge.\r\n\r\nWhen two sources conflict, follow the higher source.\r\nRecord unresolved conflicts in the NAP or the handover.\r\n\r\nBefore repeating an action, inspect committed files, `git status`,\r\n`git log`, and the relevant handover.\r\nDo not repeat a commit, submission, or external action that already succeeded.\r\n\r\nDo not retry the same failing action more than twice without new evidence.\r\nAfter two repeated failures, preserve the evidence, update the handover or\r\n`todo_inbox.md`, and escalate.\r\n\r\nClassify a failure before acting on it again:\r\n\r\n- transient tool failure: retry once with bounded output\r\n- context failure: compact or resume the same session, never relaunch\r\n- permission failure: stop and report the boundary\r\n- verification failure: diagnose and repair\r\n- maintainer decision: pause and ask, or create a proposal\r\n- unknown failure: preserve state and escalate\r\n\r\n## Interaction Contract\r\n\r\nThis section defines the shared contract.\r\nAll roles follow it.\r\nRole prompts must not redefine it.\r\n\r\n### Channels\r\n\r\nThe committed version of a file is canonical unless this table states otherwise.\r\n\r\n| Channel | Path | Written by | Read by | Canonical when |\r\n|---|---|---|---|---|\r\n| task spec | `agent/handover/handover_task.md` | planner | worker | committed |\r\n| worker summary | `agent/handover/handover_task_to_planner.md` | worker | planner | committed |\r\n| plan state / NAP | `agent/handover/handover_planner.md` | planner | next planner | committed |\r\n| action line | Last `action:` line in the planner closing message | planner | auto-resume plugin and planner | last line in message |\r\n| maintainer message | `maintainer/inbox_<role>/` | maintainer | named role | moved to `maintainer/done/` after handling |\r\n| session activity | `loop/<run>/loop_log.md` | acting roles | planner | committed |\r\n| historical work | `git log` and `archive/` summaries | roles | future sessions | committed |\r\n| current priority | `maintainer/priority.md` | maintainer | planner | committed |\r\n\r\nDo not repair one state source from another without recording the discrepancy.\r\n\r\nA Task-tool result may overwrite the worker summary after commit.\r\nThe committed file remains canonical.\r\n\r\n### Action-Line State Machine\r\n\r\nThe planner reads the final `action:` line.\r\n\r\n- `action: restart` starts a fresh planner session. This is the default.\r\n- If the action line is missing or unclear, use `action: restart`.\r\n- `action: resume` resumes the last subagent session through `task_id`. Do not reinject the task.\r\n- `action: ask_maintainer` pauses the loop until the maintainer responds.\r\n- `action: stop` ends the loop after goal completion or unrecoverable failure.\r\n\r\nIn autorun, an idle session is not a stop.\r\nAn idle agent performs its orientation's maintenance scope.\r\nIt then closes with `action: restart`.\r\n\r\nIn autorun, use `action: stop` only for unrecoverable failure or direct maintainer interaction.\r\n\r\n### Surface Rules\r\n\r\n- The summary file is the single summary channel.\r\n- The final message must point to the summary file.\r\n- The final message must not repeat the summary.\r\n- A worker never edits the NAP.\r\n- A planner never performs a worker's implementation.\r\n- Committed state is the resume contract.\r\n- A fresh session rebuilds state from files and `git log`, never from memory.\r\n\r\n## Handover Files\r\n\r\n### Plan State / NAP\r\n\r\nThe planner owns the NAP.\r\n\r\nThe NAP records:\r\n\r\n- current phase\r\n- task list and status\r\n- verification baselines\r\n- next steps\r\n- open questions\r\n\r\nKeep the NAP current whenever the planner may stop.\r\n\r\n### Task Spec\r\n\r\nThe planner writes the task spec.\r\nThe worker reads it.\r\n\r\nThe task spec contains:\r\n\r\n- goal\r\n- definition of done\r\n- approval boundary\r\n- suggested scope\r\n- assigned worker\r\n\r\nThe suggested procedure is guidance.\r\nIt is not a protocol.\r\n\r\n### Worker Summary\r\n\r\nThe worker writes the worker summary.\r\nThe planner reads it.\r\n\r\nThe worker summary contains:\r\n\r\n- change summary\r\n- measured verification\r\n- exact verification command and PASS/FAIL result\r\n- working-tree status (`clean` or `dirty`)\r\n- commit hash\r\n- `TODO.md` entries\r\n- deliberately omitted work\r\n\r\nThe worker's final message points to the summary file.\r\n\r\nSee the channel table and `repo_commands.md` in `agent/readme/` for concrete paths.\r\n\r\n## Commit Routine\r\n\r\nRun this routine before every commit.\r\n\r\nUpdate the relevant plan or handover state and `TODO.md` before committing.\r\nThis allows an interrupted agent to resume without re-exploring.\r\n\r\n### Worker Commit\r\n\r\n1. Commit one verified unit at a time.\r\n2. Include updated handover files with the final task commit.\r\n3. Apply this commit routine to every checkpoint commit.\r\n4. Ensure the working state is green before committing.\r\n\r\nAn interrupted worker loses at most one verified unit.\r\n\r\n### Planner Commit\r\n\r\n1. Update the NAP with completed work, next steps, baselines, and pending commit content.\r\n2. Update required handover files.\r\n3. Record discrepancies in `TODO.md`.\r\n4. Commit the bookkeeping with the related planner changes.\r\n\r\nA single agent performing both roles commits all required state together.\r\n\r\n### Discrepancies\r\n\r\nAppend every discrepancy to `TODO.md` as a new numbered entry.\r\n\r\nDiscrepancies include:\r\n\r\n- documentation and code mismatches\r\n- suspected bugs\r\n- stale verification baselines\r\n- inconsistent repository state\r\n\r\nThe planner curates entries:\r\n\r\n- close or condense solved entries with a one-line close note\r\n- delete only exact duplicates after preserving one entry\r\n- never silently delete open or unresolved content\r\n\r\n### Commit Rules\r\n\r\n- Commit green.\r\n- Commit often.\r\n- Never commit red.\r\n- Keep required handover files in the same commit.\r\n- Store handover files under `./agent/handover`.\r\n- Run the post-commit context check described in `Context Budget`.\r\n\r\n## Git Conventions\r\n\r\nUse a one-line imperative commit subject.\r\nName the main change.\r\n\r\nIf a commit contains multiple themes, add up to three short body lines.\r\nUse one body line per theme.\r\n\r\nDo not split commits only to improve commit-message style.\r\nThe goal is a readable `git log`.\r\n\r\nGive opportunistic fixes outside the task scope their own commits.\r\nDo not bundle them into the task commit.\r\n\r\n## Context Budget\r\n\r\nCheck the context gauge between logical work chunks and after every commit.\r\n\r\nUse the read-only command and path documented in `repo_commands.md` in `agent/readme/`.\r\nThe output has this form:\r\n\r\n```text\r\nCTX=n (p%) REM=m N Compactions left\r\n```\r\n\r\nThe injected `ctx:` value is a reminder from the last completed message.\r\nThe current gauge is authoritative.\r\n\r\nStop starting new work when either condition is true:\r\n\r\n- `REM ≤ 15k`\r\n- context usage is `≥ 90%`\r\n\r\nReserve approximately 10–15k tokens for the handover and summary.\r\n\r\nWhen the stop line is reached:\r\n\r\n1. Finish the current safe operation.\r\n2. Update the handover state.\r\n3. Finish the commit routine.\r\n4. Stop at a clean point.\r\n5. Inform the user, or compact the session before continuing.\r\n\r\nDo not start new work past the stop line.\r\n\r\nWhen the live context nudge appears, it names a rung:\r\n\r\n- `triage`: estimate the remaining calls before starting a unit\r\n- `stop`: finish the current safe operation\r\n- `emergency`: commit the current state and compact immediately\r\n- `maintainer`: report the context state to the maintainer\r\n\r\nRough call estimates: at the `triage` rung, compact when more than\r\napproximately 10 calls remain; at the `stop` rung, the threshold is\r\napproximately 6 calls.\r\n\r\nRole prompts define their per-rung actions.\r\n\r\n## Compaction Guidelines\r\n\r\nFull reference:\r\n\r\n`maintainer/draft/archive/compaction_guide/full_guide.md`\r\n\r\nThe available compaction budget is model-specific.\r\nRead the current context gauge instead of assuming fixed values.\r\n\r\n### Compaction Behavior\r\n\r\nCompaction removes older conversation history.\r\n\r\nThe last `keepMessages` messages remain intact.\r\nA same-model summary replaces the removed history.\r\n\r\nThe summary records:\r\n\r\n- session objective\r\n- important details\r\n- completed work\r\n- active work\r\n- blocked work\r\n- next move\r\n- relevant files\r\n\r\nCompaction is not a restart.\r\n\r\nAfter compaction:\r\n\r\n1. Read the NAP.\r\n2. Read relevant task and worker handover files.\r\n3. Read `TODO.md`.\r\n4. Continue from committed state.\r\n5. Do not re-plan from scratch.\r\n\r\nThe optional compaction message becomes the starting message after compaction.\r\n\r\n### Compaction Use\r\n\r\nUse normal compaction as a routine maintenance operation.\r\n\r\nCompact when:\r\n\r\n- tool output has been read and distilled\r\n- raw tool output is no longer needed\r\n- remaining work is long enough to benefit from a smaller context\r\n- context usage reaches a triage threshold\r\n\r\nAt the stop line or during a handover, compact proactively.\r\nDo not wait for automatic emergency compaction if manual compaction is available.\r\n\r\nChoose `keepMessages` to preserve information that would be expensive to recreate.\r\nPreserve drafts, rationale, in-flight work, and current plans.\r\nKeep fewer messages when committed handover files contain the required state.\r\nWhen uncertain, keep more messages.\r\n\r\n### Compaction Guardrails\r\n\r\nCommit before compacting when the current work depends on early-session knowledge.\r\nCompaction can remove that knowledge.\r\n\r\nCompaction must follow the commit.\r\nDo not compact before committing required state.\r\n\r\nThe emergency compaction uses default settings.\r\nIt may not allow custom `keepMessages` values.\r\n\r\nThe context gauge can lag actual usage by one generated turn.\r\nTreat the gauge as a lower bound.\r\n\r\nUse these triage thresholds:\r\n\r\n- At 80%, estimate whether the current work can finish safely.\r\n- At 90%, stop starting new work and compact before continuing.\r\n- At 95%, commit and compact immediately.\r\n\r\nA session dump is created during self or cross compaction.\r\nThe dump is the recovery source if the system starts a new session.\r\n\r\n## Discovery and Adjacent Fixes\r\n\r\nThese rules apply to workers and explorers.\r\n\r\n### FIX\r\n\r\nFix a small, local, confident bug within the task scope.\r\n\r\nMake the fix in the agent's own commit.\r\nAdd a one-line close note to `TODO.md`.\r\n\r\n### APPEND\r\n\r\nAppend findings that cannot be fixed confidently.\r\nAppend findings outside the task scope.\r\n\r\nUse the `submit` tool (`todo` channel) to append the finding to `todo_inbox.md`.\r\nThe planner curates the finding into `TODO.md`.\r\nThe planner assigns the stable ID during curation.\r\n\r\n### LEAVE OPEN\r\n\r\nLeave these items open in `TODO.md`:\r\n\r\n- maintainer decisions\r\n- blocked or deferred work\r\n- work that the agent must not touch\r\n\r\n### Ownership\r\n\r\nNever edit the NAP from a worker or explorer role.\r\nThe planner owns plan-state edits.\r\n\r\n### Checkpoint\r\n\r\nAfter each verified worker change, create a safe committed state.\r\nAfter each verified explorer finding, create a safe written state.\r\n\r\nA dead session must lose at most one unit of work.\r\n\r\n## Push Policy\r\n\r\nAgents never push.\r\n\r\nOnly the maintainer pushes.\r\n\r\nThe planner may record that a validated commit is ready to push.\r\n\r\n## Approval Boundaries\r\n\r\n### Pre-Approved Changes\r\n\r\nAgents may perform or delegate changes that do not alter observable behavior for the maintainer.\r\n\r\nExamples:\r\n\r\n- documentation and comments\r\n- lint fixes\r\n- dead-code removal\r\n- file and folder cleanup\r\n- metadata files\r\n- plugin and tool fixes\r\n- friction reductions used only by agents\r\n\r\n### Maintainer Approval Required\r\n\r\nObtain maintainer approval before:\r\n\r\n- changing observable behavior\r\n- removing deactivated implementations\r\n- removing commented-out alternative implementations\r\n\r\nThese changes always require a maintainer call.\r\n\r\n### Ask Early\r\n\r\nAsk the maintainer when a change is unclear and not pre-approved.\r\nDo not decide unilaterally.\r\n\r\nBundle at most two or three decisions per message.\r\nOrder decisions by priority.\r\nInclude a short recommendation for each decision.\r\n\r\n### TODO Ownership\r\n\r\n`TODO.md` contains:\r\n\r\n- open maintainer decisions\r\n- blocked or deferred work\r\n- one-line records of fixed issues\r\n\r\nDo not rediscover or recreate a fixed issue.\r\nReference the entry that the change closed.\r\n\r\n## TODO.md Entry Contract\r\n\r\nEach `TODO.md` entry must be delegable by unique ID.\r\n\r\nA handoff such as `fix #24, #30, #6` must be executable from the entries alone.\r\n\r\nEach entry must contain:\r\n\r\n- title, problem, and evidence\r\n- desired outcome, not implementation steps\r\n- acceptance criteria and definition of done\r\n- suggested file scope, which is non-exhaustive\r\n- status and decision-needed flag\r\n\r\nBare-ID handoffs are valid only when the referenced entries are self-contained.\r\n\r\nIf an entry lacks the required information, the planner adds the missing goal and definition of done to the task spec before delegation.\r\n\r\nStore closed entries in `todo_records.md`.\r\n\r\n## Access Restrictions\r\n\r\nNever circumvent an access restriction.\r\n\r\nDo not use shell, scripts, or alternate write paths around an edit deny.\r\nThe deny is the boundary.\r\n\r\nWhen a required file is inaccessible:\r\n\r\n1. Do the permitted work.\r\n2. Record the block in `handover_task_to_planner.md`.\r\n3. Close the session if the blocked file is the main task body.\r\n4. Report the block.\r\n\r\nA circumvention attempt is a prompt failure.\r\n\r\n## Command Output\r\n\r\nBefore running an unfamiliar command:\r\n\r\n1. Redirect output to a temporary file.\r\n2. Check the output size.\r\n3. Read only a bounded overview.\r\n\r\nLimit greps and similar commands.\r\nUse `head -30` or another explicit bound.\r\n\r\n## Safety Limits\r\n\r\nRepository-specific safety limits live in `repo_overview.md`.\r\n\r\nRead them before touching:\r\n\r\n- the target application\r\n- keyboard or mouse input\r\n- external services\r\n- external state\r\n- live systems\r\n- destructive operations\r\n- manual probes\r\n\r\nNever run live or destructive operations unless the repository rules explicitly permit them.\r\n\r\n## Feedback and Improvement Suggestions\r\n\r\nCheck for real friction before every closing message or handoff:\r\n\r\n- slowdown\r\n- confusion\r\n- missing context\r\n- unclear rule\r\n- near miss\r\n- tool limitation\r\n\r\nIf friction occurred, submit one actionable line per friction point.\r\nIf `submit` is unavailable, append to `agent/agent_feedback.md`.\r\nDo not create an empty feedback entry.\r\n\r\nSubmit verified role-specific lessons through the memory channel.\r\nSubmit repository-wide facts through the knowledge channel.\r\n\r\nRole prompts define the closing timing and the extra role-specific duties.\r\n\n</system-reminder>\n(21K used)"

Busy/locked on the in-process\n75: // backends (1+2): `PRAGMA busy_timeout = 2500` via the API + ONE retry;\n76: // the spawn backend keeps its own hard-timeout discipline (above).\n77: //\n78: // QUERIES — two single-row reads per read (json_extract in SQL — the message\n79: // `data` JSON is never fetched or JS-parsed):\n80: //   1. newest session:  SELECT id, model FROM session ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1\n81: //   2. finished step:   the newest session's latest message row whose `data`\n82: //      carries the `\"finish\"` marker (json_extract of tokens.total/output;\n83: //      the in-flight step has no \"finish\" field, user rows no tokens at all)\n84: // NULL total (no finished step / empty tokens) → the no-total readout.\n85: //\n86: // PER-SESSION READ (2026-09-10, TODO #30/#33 — the v2.6 nudge ladder):\n87: // readGauge(dbPath, sessionID) with a NON-EMPTY sessionID reads THAT session\n88: // instead of the newest-updated one (same two single-row reads, the session\n89: // row looked up by id, the finished step scoped to that session; the spawn\n90: // backend's marker SQL embeds the id with single-quote escaping). The\n91: // newest-session default (no/empty sessionID) is BYTE-IDENTICAL — the same\n92: // SQL strings, same order, same shapes. Result shapes are UNCHANGED; a\n93: // non-existent session reads as no-total (sid \"unknown\" — the read came from\n94: // no session row).\n95: //\n96: // TOKEN SEMANTICS (verified 2026-09-10 across all recent step rows — TODO #30;\n97: // #103, 2026-09-27 — the readout is TOTAL, not total−output):\n98: // total = input + output + cache.read holds EXACTLY ⇒ ctx = total = the\n99: // context size at the START of the NEXT turn after the latest FINISHED step\n100: // (the finished step's OWN output included). The readout thus lags the live\n101: // context by ONE generated turn (the in-flight step's output — not yet in\n102: // the DB), not TWO (the old total−output form missed the finished step's\n103: // output too). model[\"limit\"] : undefined;\n425:     const context = limit != null && typeof limit === \"object\" && !Array.isArray(limit) ? limit[\"context\"] : undefined;\n426:     if (typeof context === \"number\" && Number.isFinite(context) && context > 0) return context;\n427:   }\n428:   return parseWindow(modelId);\n429: }\n430: \n431: // Result shapes (one implementation shared by plugin + CLI + probe —\n432: // UNCHANGED by the chain; the db-error `error` text now NAMES the backend):\n433: //   ok         : { ok:true,  kind:\"ok\",        sid, modelId, total, output, ctx, window }\n434: //   no total   : { ok:false, kind:\"no-total\",  sid, modelId }            // read ok, no total\n435: //   unreadable : { ok:false, kind:\"db-error\",  sid:\"unknown\", modelId:\"\", error }\n436: \n437: // Item 3 (2026-09-24): the \"N compactions left\" suffix — the compaction\n438: // budget remaining for the READ session, appended as a DISTINCT field to the\n439: // readout line (format ` | N compactions left`, singular at 1; the budget\n440: // THRESHOLD is deliberately NOT revealed — indirect information only).\n441: // remaining = max(0, cap - count) PLUS 1 iff the once-per-session emergency\n442: // compaction is still available: count === cap && the effective\n443: // emergency_budget >= 1. eb : 1; // absent → fail-open default 1\n496:   const remaining = Math.max(0, cap - count) + (count === cap && emergency >= 1 ? 1 : 0);\n497:   return ` | ${remaining} compaction${remaining === 1 ? \"\" : \"s\"} left${unlisted ? \" (unlisted)\" : \"\"}`;\n498: }\n499: \n500: // The ONE readout form (see the header). sid missing/undefined -> \"unknown\".\n501: // Item 3 (2026-09-24): the line ends with the budget suffix when the store\n502: // resolves (compactionsLeftSuffix — empty string on fail-open).\n503: export function formatGauge(r) {\n504:   let out;\n505:   if (r.ok) {\n506:     const w = r.window;\n507:     out =\n508:       w == null || w <= 0\n509:         ? \"unknown\"} ${out}` + compactionsLeftSuffix(r.sid ?? \"unknown\", r.modelId ?? \"\");\n515: }\n516: \n517: // The two single-row reads (see the header). json_extract never fetches the\n518: // `data` JSON into JS; a missing/NULL token field reads as null.\n519: // v2-migration unit 1 (2026-10-21): the SESSION-ROW leg is TABLE-AWARE — the\n520: // v2 line stores sessions in `session_v2` (columns = `session` PLUS the fork/\n521: // idle columns; the `model` column NAME unchanged, its value JSON `{\"id\":…}`\n522: // — parseModelId already unwraps both shapes); a migrated DB carries BOTH\n523: // tables (coexistence measured 2026-10-21). The leg reads `session_v2` FIRST,\n524: // then `session`; on a v1-only DB the v1 SQL below goes out BYTE-IDENTICAL\n525: // (the constants are the `session` instantiation of the table templates).\n526: // Newest-session choice across BOTH tables = the greater `time_updated`.\n527: // v2-migration unit 2 (2026-10-22): the TOKEN-mass leg is TABLE-AWARE the\n528: // SAME WAY — its SQL is instantiated against THE TABLE THAT WON THE SESSION-\n529: // ROW PICK (no cross-table mass pairing). The v2 line stores per-assistant-\n530: // message usage in the `session_message.data` blob: `tokens: {input, output,\n531: // reasoning, cache: {read, write}}` (@opencode/schema/token-usage.ts +\n532: // session-message.ts `Assistant`; v1-migration.bun.ts writes the same shape;\n533: // verified 2026-10-22 with one mode=ro read of the live migrated DB — real\n534: // rows carry e.g. input 72 / output 176 / cache.read 14283). The mass TOTAL\n535: // mirrors the fork's OWN TokenUsage.total() = input+output+reasoning+cache.\n536: // read+cache.write — the #103 in+out+cr semantics of v1's `tokens.total`\n537: // mapped to what v2 actually stores (llama-swap usage keeps reasoning/cache.\n538: // write at 0, so the sum lands where v1's total landed). FINISHED-STEP\n539: // discriminator = the fork's own completed-assistant filter (projector.ts\n540: // fork-copy): `type='assistant' AND $.time.completed IS NOT NULL AND $.tokens\n541: // IS NOT NULL` — an in-flight assistant row has neither; the `type` match\n542: // keeps `compaction` rows (their request usage, NOT the context size) out.\n543: // The v1 `message`/`session` instantiation below stays BYTE-IDENTICAL — the\n544: // existing gauge_core pins (v1-only DBs) are unchanged.\n545: const sqlSessionNewest = (t) => `SELECT id, model FROM ${t} ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1`;\n546: const sqlSessionById = (t) => `SELECT id, model FROM ${t} WHERE id = ?`;\n547: const SQL_NEWEST_SESSION = sqlSessionNewest(\"session\");\n548: const SQL_FINISHED_STEP =\n549:   \"SELECT s.id AS sid, s.model AS model, \" +\n550:   \"json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.total') AS total, \" +\n551:   \"json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.output') AS output \" +\n552:   \"FROM message m JOIN session s ON s.id = m.session_id \" +\n553:   \"WHERE s.id = (SELECT id FROM session ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1) \" +\n554:   'AND m.data LIKE \\'%\"finish\"%\\' ORDER BY m.time_created DESC LIMIT 1';\n555: \n556: // v2-migration unit 2 (2026-10-22): the v2 instantiation of the same mass\n557: // read (see the TABLE-AWARE header). TOTAL = the fork's own TokenUsage.total()\n558: // sum; discriminator = the fork's completed-assistant filter; the v2 line has\n559: // NO `tokens.total` field — the five-field sum IS the v2 storage (verified\n560: // against @opencode/schema/token-usage.ts, the v1→v2 migration, and one\n561: // mode=ro read of the live migrated DB).\n562: const SQL_STEP_DISCRIMINATOR_V2 =\n563:   \"m.type = 'assistant' AND json_extract(m.data, '$.time.completed') IS NOT NULL AND json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens') IS NOT NULL\";\n564: const SQL_TOKEN_TOTAL_V2 =\n565:   \"(COALESCE(json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.input'), 0) + \" +\n566:   \"COALESCE(json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.output'), 0) + \" +\n567:   \"COALESCE(json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.reasoning'), 0) + \" +\n568:   \"COALESCE(json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.cache.read'), 0) + \" +\n569:   \"COALESCE(json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.cache.write'), 0))\";\n570: const SQL_FINISHED_STEP_V2 =\n571:   \"SELECT s.id AS sid, s.model AS model, \" +\n572:   `${SQL_TOKEN_TOTAL_V2} AS total, ` +\n573:   \"json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.output') AS output \" +\n574:   \"FROM session_message m JOIN session_v2 s ON s.id = m.session_id \" +\n575:   \"WHERE s.id = (SELECT id FROM session_v2 ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1) \" +\n576:   `AND ${SQL_STEP_DISCRIMINATOR_V2} ORDER BY m.time_created DESC LIMIT 1`;\n577: \n578: // The spawn backend's MARKER SQL (v1.x discipline — see the header): two\n579: // statements, marker-prefixed rows; an empty first result simply omits the M\n580: // row, so the output is never ambiguous.\n581: const GAUGE_SQL_MARKER =\n582:   \"SELECT 'M', s.id, s.model, json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.total'), json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.output') \" +\n583:   \"FROM message m JOIN session s ON s.id = m.session_id \" +\n584:   \"WHERE s.id = (SELECT id FROM session ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1) \" +\n585:   \"AND m.data LIKE '%\\\"finish\\\"%' ORDER BY m.time_created DESC LIMIT 1; \" +\n586:   \"SELECT 'S', id, model FROM session ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1;\";\n587: \n588: // v2 unit 2: the spawn backend's v2 marker — SAME 5-field M row + 2-field S\n589: // row shape (the shared parser handles both forms; the fork's JSON `{\"id\":…}`\n590: // model value unwraps via parseModelId). Instantiated only when `session_v2`\n591: // exists (SQL_TABLE_PROBE — the spawn backend's table probe, one cheap first\n592: // exec deciding which marker form goes out; the v1 marker above stays the\n593: // choice for v1-only DBs, byte-identical).\n594: const GAUGE_SQL_MARKER_V2 =\n595:   \"SELECT 'M', s.id, s.model, \" +\n596:   `${SQL_TOKEN_TOTAL_V2}, json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.output') ` +\n597:   \"FROM session_message m JOIN session_v2 s ON s.id = m.session_id \" +\n598:   \"WHERE s.id = (SELECT id FROM session_v2 ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1) \" +\n599:   `AND ${SQL_STEP_DISCRIMINATOR_V2} ORDER BY m.time_created DESC LIMIT 1; ` +\n600:   \"SELECT 'S', id, model FROM session_v2 ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1;\";\n601: const SQL_TABLE_PROBE =\n602:   \"SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type = 'table' AND name IN ('session_v2','session','session_message','message')\";\n603: \n604: // v2.6 — the PER-SESSION query forms (the ladder's blind-spot-free read; see\n605: // the header block). Same column list and LIKE marker as the newest-session\n606: // forms; the session is pinned by id (in-process: bound parameter; spawn:\n607: // literal with single-quote escaping — opencode session ids are `ses_…`, the\n608: // escape is belt-and-braces, not a quoting surface for user input).\n609: const SQL_SESSION_BY_ID = sqlSessionById(\"session\");\n610: const SQL_FINISHED_STEP_SESSION =\n611:   \"SELECT s.id AS sid, s.model AS model, \" +\n612:   \"json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.total') AS total, \" +\n613:   \"json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.output') AS output \" +\n614:   \"FROM message m JOIN session s ON s.id = m.session_id \" +\n615:   \"WHERE s.id = ? \" +\n616:   'AND m.data LIKE \\'%\"finish\"%\\' ORDER BY m.time_created DESC LIMIT 1';\n617: const SQL_FINISHED_STEP_SESSION_V2 =\n618:   \"SELECT s.id AS sid, s.model AS model, \" +\n619:   `${SQL_TOKEN_TOTAL_V2} AS total, ` +\n620:   \"json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.output') AS output \" +\n621:   \"FROM session_message m JOIN session_v2 s ON s.id = m.session_id \" +\n622:   \"WHERE s.id = ? \" +\n623:   `AND ${SQL_STEP_DISCRIMINATOR_V2} ORDER BY m.time_created DESC LIMIT 1`;\n624: const sqlSidLiteral = (sid) => `'${String(sid).replace(/'/g, \"''\")}'`;\n625: const sqlMarkerForSession = (sid) => {\n626:   const q = sqlSidLiteral(sid);\n627:   return (\n628:     \"SELECT 'M', s.id, s.model, json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.total'), json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.output') \" +\n629:     \"FROM message m JOIN session s ON s.id = m.session_id \" +\n630:     `WHERE s.id = ${q} AND m.data LIKE '%\"finish\"%' ORDER BY m.time_created DESC LIMIT 1; ` +\n631:     `SELECT 'S', id, model FROM session WHERE id = ${q};`\n632:   );\n633: };\n634: // v2 unit 2: the v2 per-session spawn marker — SAME field order as the v1\n635: // form (the shared parser + the v2-only instantiation; the coexisting-DB\n636: // winner pick reuses this builder pinned to the WINNER's sid — pairing by\n637: // construction).\n638: const sqlMarkerV2ForSession = (sid) => {\n639:   const q = sqlSidLiteral(sid);\n640:   return (\n641:     \"SELECT 'M', s.id, s.model, \" +\n642:     `${SQL_TOKEN_TOTAL_V2}, json_extract(m.data, '$.tokens.output') ` +\n643:     \"FROM session_message m JOIN session_v2 s ON s.id = m.session_id \" +\n644:     `WHERE s.id = ${q} AND ${SQL_STEP_DISCRIMINATOR_V2} ORDER BY m.time_created DESC LIMIT 1; ` +\n645:     `SELECT 'S', id, model FROM session_v2 WHERE id = ${q};`\n646:   );\n647: };\n648: \n649: // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n650: // The backends — each returns the SAME raw shape:\n651: //   { sid: string|undefined, model: string|null, total: number|null, output: number|null }\n652: // and THROWS on any hard failure (the chain maps that to the named db-error).\n653: \n654: // In-process helper shared by backends 1+2: open (READ-ONLY), the busy_timeout\n655: // PRAGMA via the API, the two single-row reads, ONE retry on busy/locked,\n656: // best-effort close. `target` (v2.6): the per-session read — when set, the\n657: // session row is looked up by id and the finished step is scoped to it;\n658: // unset keeps the newest-session SQL byte-identical.\n659: // v2-migration unit 1 (2026-10-21): the SESSION-ROW leg resolves through\n660: // `sessionRow` — `session_v2` FIRST, then `session` (both tables coexist in a\n661: // migrated DB; NEITHER present keeps the v1 hard-failure outcome). Unit 2\n662: // (2026-10-22): `sessionRow` ALSO REPORTS the winning TABLE — the mass leg\n663: // then runs the instantiation PAIRED with it (session_v2↔session_message,\n664: // session↔message); a MISSING PAIRED message table degrades to null (no-total)\n665: // instead of failing the whole read.\n666: const sessionTablesIn = (db) => {\n667:   const found = [];\n668:   for (const t of [\"session_v2\", \"session\"]) {\n669:     try {\n670:       const row = db.prepare(\"SELECT 1 AS x FROM sqlite_master WHERE type = 'table' AND name = ?\").get(t);\n671:       if (row != null) found.push(t);\n672:     } catch {\n673:       // unreadable catalog — the v1 table name is the only sane fallback\n674:     }\n675:   }\n676:   return found;\n677: };\n678: const sessionRow = (db, target) => {\n679:   const tables = sessionTablesIn(db);\n680:   if (tables.length === 0) throw new Error(\"no session table\");\n681:   if (target != null) {\n682:     for (const t of tables) {\n683:       const row = db.prepare(sqlSessionById(t)).get(target);\n684:       if (row != null) return { table: t, row }; // an id present in BOTH: session_v2 wins\n685:     }\n686:     return { table: undefined, row: undefined };\n687:   }\n688:   let best;\n689:   let bestTable;\n690:   for (const t of tables) {\n691:     const row = db.prepare(sqlSessionNewest(t)).get();\n692:     if (row == null) continue;\n693:     if (best === undefined) {\n694:       best = row;\n695:       bestTable = t;\n696:       continue;\n697:     }\n698:     if (tables.length < 2) continue; // single-table DB: no cross-table compare\n699:     const rn = Number(db.prepare(`SELECT time_updated FROM ${t} WHERE id = ?`).get(row.id)?.time_updated ?? -Infinity);\n700:     const rb = Number(db.prepare(`SELECT time_updated FROM ${bestTable} WHERE id = ?`).get(best.id)?.time_updated ?? -Infinity);\n701:     if (rn > rb) {\n702:       best = row;\n703:       bestTable = t;\n704:     } // a tie keeps the FIRST (`session_v2`) row\n705:   }\n706:   return { table: bestTable, row: best };\n707: };\n708: function readApiDb(openFn, target) {\n709:   const attempt = () => {\n710:     let db;\n711:     try {\n712:       db = openFn();\n713:       db.exec(`PRAGMA busy_timeout = ${BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS};`);\n714:       const picked = sessionRow(db, target);\n715:       const newest = picked.row;\n716:       const massV2 = picked.table === \"session_v2\";\n717:       let step = null;\n718:       try {\n719:         step =\n720:           target != null\n721:             ? db.prepare(massV2 ? SQL_FINISHED_STEP_SESSION_V2 : SQL_FINISHED_STEP_SESSION).get(target)\n722:             : db.prepare(massV2 ? SQL_FINISHED_STEP_V2 : SQL_FINISHED_STEP).get();\n723:       } catch (e) {\n724:         // unit 2: the mass leg is instantiated against the PAIRED tables of\n725:         // the SESSION-ROW winner (session_v2↔session_message; session↔message\n726:         // — the v1 instantiation goes out BYTE-IDENTICAL). A MISSING PAIRED\n727:         // message table degrades to null (no-total); any other error\n728:         // propagates.\n729:         if (!/no such table/i.test(String(e?.message ?? e))) throw e;\n730:       }\n731:       return {\n732:         sid: typeof newest?.id === \"string\" && newest.id !== \"\" ? newest.id : undefined,\n733:         model: step != null && typeof step.model === \"string\" ? step.model : (typeof newest?.model === \"string\" ? newest.model : null),\n734:         total: step?.total == null ? null : Number(step.total),\n735:         output: step?.output == null ? null : Number(step.output),\n736:       };\n737:     } finally {\n738:       try {\n739:         db?.close();\n740:       } catch {\n741:         // best effort — the read outcome has already been captured\n742:       }\n743:     }\n744:   };\n745:   try {\n746:     return attempt();\n747:   } catch (e) {\n748:     if (/busy|locked/i.test(String(e?.message ?? e))) {\n749:       // busy/locked on the first attempt (opencode writes concurrently) and\n750:       // the busy_timeout budget exhausted: retry ONCE before giving up.\n751:       return attempt();\n752:     }\n753:     throw e;\n754:   }\n755: }\n756: \n757: // Backend 1 — node:sqlite (DatabaseSync, readOnly). Flag-free on the system\n758: // node v24.19.0; also exposed by the SYSTEM bun 1.4.2 (NOT the opencode host).\n759: async function readNodeSqlite(p, target) {\n760:   const mod = await importModule(\"node:sqlite\");\n761:   const DatabaseSync = mod?.DatabaseSync;\n762:   if (typeof DatabaseSync !== \"function\") throw new Error(\"DatabaseSync export missing\");\n763:   return readApiDb(() => new DatabaseSync(p, { readOnly: true }), target);\n764: }\n765: \n766: // Backend 2 — bun:sqlite (the native bun module). API facts verified on the\n767: // system bun 1.4.2 — see the READ BACKEND CHAIN header (the spec's\n768: // `{ create: false, readWrite: false }` sketch was WRONG for bun 1.4.2:\n769: // the option is `readonly`, unknown options are rejected).\n770: async function readBunSqlite(p, target) {\n771:   const mod = await importModule(\"bun:sqlite\");\n772:   const Database = mod?.Database ?? (typeof mod?.default === \"function\" ? mod.default : mod?.default?.Database);\n773:   if (typeof Database !== \"function\") throw new Error(\"Database export missing\");\n774:   const open = () => {\n775:     try {\n776:       return new Database(p, { readonly: true, timeout: BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS });\n777:     } catch (e) {\n778:       // shape-surprise fallback: a host whose bun rejects the combined\n779:       // options falls back to the plain readonly form — but ONLY for a\n780:       // constructor shape error; an OPEN error (missing file, …) propagates.\n781:       if (/bad parameter|api misuse|misspelled/i.test(String(e?.message ?? e))) return new Database(p, { readonly: true });\n782:       throw e;\n783:     }\n784:   };\n785:   return readApiDb(open, target);\n786: }\n787: \n788: // Backend 3 — spawn-sqlite3 (the v1.x discipline, recovered from git\n789: // history 2cf5f33; see the READ BACKEND CHAIN header for the full rationale).\n790: const uriRo = (p) => `file:${p.replace(/\\\\/g, \"/\")}?mode=ro`;\n791: // The sqlite3 CLI prints NULL as an EMPTY field (and honors a NULLVALUE of\n792: // \"NULL\" only if configured) — treat both as \"no value\" → no-total. (The\n793: // v1.x `Number(\"\") → 0` would have misread that as total 0 — hardened here.)\n794: const parseMarkerNumber = (v) => {\n795:   if (typeof v !== \"string\" || v === \"\" || v === \"NULL\") return null;\n796:   const n = Number(v);\n797:   return Number.isNaN(n) ? null : n;\n798: };\n799: // v2 unit 2 (2026-10-22): the spawn read is TABLE-AWARE via a cheap probe\n800: // exec FIRST (SQL_TABLE_PROBE — sqlite_master names only; a missing/unopenable\n801: // DB fails here exactly like the single-exec v1 form failed before, so the\n802: // db-error shape and the \"deepest failing backend\" pin survive). Then ONE\n803: // marker exec, instantiated by the probe:\n804: // - `session_v2` ABSENT  → the v1 marker constants, BYTE-IDENTICAL (the\n805: //   existing probe spawn pins 34/35/36 ride this path unchanged);\n806: // - `session_v2` present, no legacy `session` table (or legacy rows none) →\n807: //   the v2 marker constants;\n808: // - COEXISTING session tables → a `W` candidate exec (newest row per table\n809: //   + time_updated) picks the WINNER in JS — the SAME rule as the in-process\n810: //   sessionRow (greater time_updated; a tie keeps session_v2); the marker\n811: //   exec is then PINNED to the winner's sid via the per-session builders —\n812: //   mass pairing by construction (DoD 3: no cross-table pairing). A per-\n813: //   session target skips the candidate exec: the table HOLDING the id wins\n814: //   (session_v2 when present in BOTH).\n815: // NO session table → throw (\"no session table\") — the v1 hard-failure outcome\n816: // shape (the marker exec would have died on \"no such table\" before).\n817: const spawnExec = (p, sql) =>\n818:   execFileAsync(DEFAULT_EXE_PATH, [uriRo(p), sql], {\n819:     timeout: SPAWN_TIMEOUT_MS, // hard KILL — a stuck child becomes a db-error, never a hang\n820:     maxBuffer: MAX_BUFFER,\n821:     encoding: \"utf8\",\n822:   }).then(\n823:     (res) => res,\n824:     (e) => {\n825:       const isTimeout = e?.killed === true || /timed?\\s*out/i.test(String(e?.message ?? \"\"));\n826:       const detail = isTimeout ? `timeout ${SPAWN_TIMEOUT_MS}ms kill` : String(e?.stderr ?? \"\").trim() || String(e?.message ?? e);\n827:       throw new Error(String(detail).replace(/\\r?\\n+/g, \" | \").slice(0, 106));\n828:     },\n829:   );\n830: async function readSpawnSqlite3(p, target) {\n831:   if (!existsSync(DEFAULT_EXE_PATH)) throw new Error(`exe-missing ${DEFAULT_EXE_PATH}`);\n832:   const tables = new Set(\n833:     String((await spawnExec(p, SQL_TABLE_PROBE)).stdout)\n834:       .split(/\\r?\\n/)\n835:       .map((x) => x.trim())\n836:       .filter((x) => x !== \"\"),\n837:   );\n838:   const hasV2 = tables.has(\"session_v2\");\n839:   const hasV1 = tables.has(\"session\");\n840:   if (!hasV2 && !hasV1) throw new Error(\"no session table\");\n841:   // Winner pick among the session tables — the SAME rule as the in-process\n842:   // sessionRow (session_v2 FIRST; coexisting greater time_updated wins, a\n843:   // tie keeps session_v2; an id TARGET is held by its table, session_v2 wins\n844:   // in BOTH). The marker exec is then PINNED to the winner's table+sid (DoD\n845:   // 3: the mass pairs with the SAME table that won the session-row pick);\n846:   // an empty winSid means \"no rows / single table\" → the subquery form.\n847:   let winTable;\n848:   let winSid;\n849:   const spawnHeld = async (t, sid) => {\n850:     const out = String((await spawnExec(p, `SELECT 'Y' FROM ${t} WHERE id = ${sqlSidLiteral(sid)} LIMIT 1;`)).stdout);\n851:     return out.split(/\\r?\\n/).some((x) => x.trim() === \"Y\");\n852:   };\n853:   if (target != null) {\n854:     winSid = String(target);\n855:     if (hasV2) {\n856:       if (await spawnHeld(\"session_v2\", target)) winTable = \"session_v2\";\n857:       else if (hasV1 && await spawnHeld(\"session\", target)) winTable = \"session\";\n858:       else winTable = \"session_v2\"; // in NEITHER: empty rows via the v2 pair (no-total shape)\n859:     } else {\n860:       winTable = \"session\"; // v1-only DB: today's per-session form\n861:     }\n862:   } else if (hasV2 && !hasV1) {\n863:     winTable = \"session_v2\";\n864:     winSid = \"\";\n865:   } else if (!hasV2) {\n866:     winTable = \"session\";\n867:     winSid = \"\"; // v1-only DB: today's newest form, BYTE-IDENTICAL\n868:   } else {\n869:     // coexisting newest form: one `W` candidate exec resolves the winner.\n870:     const rows = String(\n871:       (\n872:         await spawnExec(\n873:           p,\n874:           \"SELECT 'W', id, time_updated FROM session_v2 ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1; SELECT 'W', id, time_updated FROM session ORDER BY time_updated DESC LIMIT 1;\",\n875:         )\n876:       ).stdout,\n877:     );\n878:     let wV2;\n879:     let wV1;\n880:     for (const line of rows.split(/\\r?\\n/)) {\n881:       if (!line.startsWith(\"W|\")) continue;\n882:       const parts = line.slice(2).split(\"|\");\n883:       const tnum = Number(parts[parts.length - 1]);\n884:       const rec = { sid: parts[0], t: Number.isFinite(tnum) ? tnum : -Infinity };\n885:       if (wV2 === undefined) wV2 = rec;\n886:       else if (wV1 === undefined) wV1 = rec;\n887:     }\n888:     if (wV2 !== undefined && !(wV1 !== undefined && wV1.t > wV2.t)) {\n889:       winTable = \"session_v2\";\n890:       winSid = String(wV2.sid);\n891:     } else {\n892:       winTable = \"session\";\n893:       winSid = String(wV1?.sid ?? \"\");\n894:     }\n895:   }\n896:   const markerSql =\n897:     winTable === \"session_v2\"\n898:       ? winSid === \"\"\n899:         ?   [tool] bash callID=call_efff461665cb431aab501779 status=completed

  [tool output] "// The U1 recommendation block: the 3 retention options (low / medium /\n// high), each anchored at a MESSAGE ID (stable across pre/post-compaction\n// deliberation — the row number shifts, the id doesn't), each with its\n// keep count (the tail-mode keepMessages value), the mass (the spec's\n// massOf rule) + the read/thought/shell split of retained vs dropped, and\n// the oldest-kept (anchor) message's one-line hint. retained = anchor →\n// just before the compaction row + the always-in-window post-summary\n// slice; dropped = strictly before the anchor; the marker + summary pair\n// is in neither (they never sit in the model window). low = the current\n// tail head (the status-quo boundary — no write needed); high = the\n// earliest floor-valid anchor (index 0, when the pre-compaction history\n// leaves >= FLOOR messages — else omitted); medium = the midpoint index\n// (omitted when it coincides with an end). Emitted only for a proper\n// completed-compaction window (hostGuard + the strict summary).\nfunction retentionOptions(w: WindowState, msgs: Msg[]): string[] {\n  if (!(w.hostGuard && w.strictSummaryIndex >= 0)) return [];\n  const postStart = w.summaryIndex + 1;\n  const opts: { idx: number; label: string }[] = [{ idx: w.tailIndex, label: \"low\" }];\n  const highIdx = w.compactionIndex >= FLOOR ? 0 : -1;\n  if (highIdx >= 0) {\n    const mid = Math.floor((w.tailIndex + highIdx) / 2);\n    if (mid !== w.tailIndex && mid !== highIdx) opts.push({ idx: mid, label: \"medium\" });\n    if (highIdx !== w.tailIndex) opts.push({ idx: highIdx, label: \"high\" });\n  }\n  const out: string[] = [];\n  for (const o of opts) {\n    const retainedSet = [...msgs.slice(o.idx, w.compactionIndex), ...msgs.slice(postStart)];\n    const droppedSet = msgs.slice(0, o.idx);\n    const keep = w.compactionIndex - o.idx;\n    const rSplit = splitOf(retainedSet);\n    const dSplit = splitOf(droppedSet);\n    const rMass = retainedSet.reduce((a, m) => a + massOf(m), 0);\n    const dMass = droppedSet.reduce((a, m) => a + massOf(m), 0);\n    out.push(\n      `option ${o.label}: msg=${msgs[o.idx].id} keep=${keep}` +\n        ` retained=${rMass} (read=${rSplit.read} thought=${rSplit.thought} shell=${rSplit.shell})` +\n        ` dropped=${dMass} (read=${dSplit.read} thought=${dSplit.thought} shell=${dSplit.shell})` +\n        ` hint=${hintOf(msgs[o.idx])}`,\n    );\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n\n// The U1 biggest-droppable list: the top-N heaviest COMPLETED assistant\n// turns (finished, no error — a \"call-result pair\" is such a turn\n// carrying its tool parts' inputs + outputs) in the window, as window row\n// number + mass (the U2 filter's target list). Tie → the lower row\n// number first.\nfunction biggestDroppable(windowMsgs: Msg[], top: number): { row: number; mass: number }[] {\n  const turns: { row: number; mass: number }[] = [];\n  windowMsgs.forEach((m, i) => {\n    if (m.data?.role !== \"assistant\" || !m.data?.finish || m.data?.error) return;\n    turns.push({ row: i + 1, mass: massOf(m) });\n  });\n  turns.sort((a, b) => b.mass - a.mass || a.row - b.row);\n  return turns.slice(0, top);\n}\n\n// U4 (2026-10-01 context-budget package): the triage rung's recommendation\n// one-liner — the U1 recommendation block in compact form (the\n// auto_resume nudge's `--triage` line): the 3 retention options (low /\n// medium / high, the block's order) as `keep from <N> ≈<K>k | ...`\n// (N = the anchor message's 1-based position in the session's message\n// list — the anchors can sit BEFORE the window (medium/high extend the\n// retained tail), so it is the full-session number, not a window row;\n// K = the retained mass in whole k, rounded) + the biggest-droppable\n// single turn as `drop turn <row> (≈<K>k)` (the U1 window row number +\n// the total mass). Options + drop join on `; `. No proper\n// completed-compaction window → no options part; no finished turn → no\n// drop part; neither → \"\" (the caller emits the bare triage line).\n// READ-ONLY (no write statement is ever executed). Session missing /\n// db-error → \"\" (a nudge is never blocked by a recommendation failure).\nexport async function recommendationOneLiner(dbPath: string, sessionID: string): Promise<string> {\n  let ctx: Ctx;\n  try {\n    ctx = await openDb(dbPath);\n  } catch {\n    return \"\";\n  }\n  try {\n    const loaded = await loadSession(ctx, sessionID);\n    if (!loaded.exists) return \"\";\n    const msgs = loaded.msgs;\n    const w = computeWindow(msgs);\n    const parts: string[] = [];\n    // The options (the anchor choice mirrors the U1 block exactly).\n    if (w.hostGuard && w.strictSummaryIndex >= 0) {\n      const opts: number[] = [w.tailIndex];\n      const highIdx = w.compactionIndex >= FLOOR ? 0 : -1;\n      if (highIdx >= 0) {\n        const mid = Math.floor((w.tailIndex + highIdx) / 2);\n        if (mid !== w.tailIndex && mid !== highIdx) opts.push(mid);\n        if (highIdx !== w.tailIndex) opts.push(highIdx);\n      }\n      const postStart = w.summaryIndex + 1;\n      parts.push(\n        \"options: keep from \" +\n          opts\n            .map((idx) => {\n              const retained = [...msgs.slice(idx, w.compactionIndex), ...msgs.slice(postStart)];\n              const mass = retained.reduce((a, m) => a + massOf(m), 0);\n              return `${idx + 1} ≈${Math.round(mass / 1000)}k`;\n            })\n            .join(\" | \"),\n      );\n    }\n    // The biggest-droppable single turn (the U1 rule, top-1).\n    const windowMsgs: Msg[] = w.hostGuard\n      ? [...msgs.slice(w.tailIndex, w.compactionIndex), ...msgs.slice(w.summaryIndex + 1)]\n      : [...msgs];\n    const big = biggestDroppable(windowMsgs, 1)[0];\n    if (big) parts.push(`drop turn ${big.row} (≈${Math.round(big.mass / 1000)}k)`);\n    return parts.join(\"; \");\n  } catch {\n    return \"\";\n  } finally {\n    try {\n      ctx.db.close();\n    } catch {\n      // best effort — the read outcome has already been captured\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// reportWindow (READ-ONLY — no write statement is ever executed).\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// `opts.brief` = the U1 cut-down form (numbered rows + `last: N` + the\n// recommendation block + the biggest-droppable list) — the cheap default\n// the TOOL SURFACE returns; omitted/false → the FULL form (byte-identical\n// to the pre-U1 shape — the existing pins' contract). `opts.top` = the\n// biggest-droppable list size (default 5 — the surface passes no opts).\nexport async function reportWindow(\n  dbPath: string,\n  sessionID: string,\n  target?: string,\n  opts?: { brief?: boolean; top?: number },\n): Promise<string> {\n  let ctx: Ctx;\n  try {\n    ctx = await openDb(dbPath);\n  } catch (e: any) {\n    return `db-error: ${String(e?.message ?? e).replace(/\\r?\\n+/g, \" | \").slice(0, 120)}`;\n  }\n  try {\n    const loaded = await loadSession(ctx, sessionID);\n    if (!loaded.exists) return `session ${sessionID}\\nreport= rejected: session-not-found`;\n    const msgs = loaded.msgs;\n    const w = computeWindow(msgs);\n    const brief = opts?.brief === true;\n    const top = opts?.top != null && Number.isFinite(opts.top) ? w.tailIndex : w.compactionIndex; // degenerate → no effective retained tail\n        const removeCount = targetIndex > oldStart ? targetIndex - oldStart : 0;\n        const removeMass = msgs\n          .slice(oldStart, targetIndex > oldStart ? targetIndex : oldStart)\n          .reduce((a, m) => a + massOf(m), 0);\n        const keepCount = w.compactionIndex - targetIndex;\n        const keepMass = msgs.slice(targetIndex, w.compactionIndex).reduce((a, m) => a + massOf(m), 0);\n        dry = `dry-run: target=${target} remove=${removeCount} (${removeMass} tokens) keep=${keepCount} (${keepMass} tokens) verdict=${verdict}`;\n      } else {\n        dry = `dry-run: target=${target} verdict=${verdict}`;\n      }\n      lines.push(dry);\n    }\n    return lines.join(\"\\n\");\n  } catch (e: any) {\n    return `session ${sessionID}\\nerror: ${String(e?.message ?? e).replace(/\\r?\\n+/g, \" | \").slice(0, 120)}`;\n  } finally {\n    try {\n      ctx.db.close();\n    } catch {\n      // best effort — the read outcome has already been captured\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// The dry-run verdict = the tail-write validation (fail-closed, mirrors\n// the guard) — `ok` or `rejected: <reason>`.\nfunction dryRunVerdict(w: WindowState, msgs: Msg[], target: string): string {\n  if (w.compactionIndex < 0 || w.strictSummaryIndex < 0) return \"rejected: no-completed-compaction\";\n  const targetIndex = msgs.findIndex((m) => m.id === target);\n  if (targetIndex < 0) return \"rejected: target-not-found\";\n  if (targetIndex >= w.compactionIndex) return \"rejected: target-not-before-compaction\";\n  const retained = w.compactionIndex - targetIndex;\n  if (retained < FLOOR) return `rejected: retained-tail-below-floor ${retained}`;\n  return \"ok\";\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// tailSet (WRITE — single transaction; WAL; one busy retry; fail-closed).\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport async function tailSet(dbPath: string, sessionID: string, targetID: string): Promise<string> {\n  let ctx: Ctx;\n  try {\n    ctx = await openDb(dbPath);\n  } catch (e: any) {\n    return `tail= db-error: ${String(e?.message ?? ORDER BY time_created ASC, id ASC\")\n    .all(sessionID);\n  const pRows: any[] = await ctx.db\n    .prepare(\"SELECT id, message_id, data FROM part WHERE session_id = ? ORDER BY message_id ASC, id ASC\")\n    .all(sessionID);\n  const partsByMsg = new Map<string, Part[]>();\n  for (const r of pRows) {\n    const list = partsByMsg.get(r.message_id);\n    const part: Part = { id: r.id, data: parseJsonSafe(String(r.data), `part ${r.id}`) };\n    if (list) list.push(part);\n    else partsByMsg.set(r.message_id, [part]);\n  }\n  const msgs: Msg[] = mRows.map((r) => ({\n    id: r.id,\n    time: Number(r.time_created),\n    data: parseJsonSafe(String(r.data), `message ${r.id}`),\n    parts: partsByMsg.get(r.id) ?? I'm understanding the v2 data model for session messages, where each row contains an ID, session reference, message type (user/assistant/system and others), sequence number, timestamps, and a data JSON structure that varies by type—with token counts and tool details nested inside for assistants, text fields for user messages, and completion metadata tracked at the row level.

For the report design in v2, I'm thinking about using a SQL query to load the session's message rows ordered by sequence, then computing the window in JavaScript by finding the last completed compaction row, which determines what falls into the current model context window. The spec clarifies that the window starts after the last compaction marker, and if no marker exists, the full history becomes the window. I need to check the actual handler implementation and the compaction logic to see exactly whether the compaction row itself gets included in the context sent to the model, since that affects whether the window boundaries should include or exclude that row.

## msg_1622112dc001yo72WMCKEZ54tG | role=assistant | agent=worker_FlashNext | model=qwen3.8-flash-next-coder-iq1_m | 2026-10-22T05:41:37.116Z

The key finding is that history loads from the latest completed compaction, making that compaction row the first message in the loaded array. This means the v2 model window spans from that last completed compaction row onward, inclusive. The compaction row's summary and recent text fields become the first entry, so for the report I need to include it as row 1 and calculate the window accordingly.

Now I'm thinking through the validation boundary: it must sit strictly after the compaction row, leaving at least 6 messages after it. Since the window includes the compaction row itself, the retained tail equals the total window length minus the boundary index. For the report header in v2, I'm adapting the format to show the marker (the compaction row's id and status), the tail (the current boundary from the store or "none"), and the counts (window size in messages). The rows themselves mirror the v1 brief form, displaying type, mass label, and tool-target details from content parts, but in v2 a single assistant row carries its tool calls directly rather than having separate part rows.

For token mass calculation, I'm reusing the gauge core's sum formula when tokens are present and completed—summing input, output, reasoning, and cache read/write values. The mass discriminator uses `$.time.completed IS NOT NULL` to identify finished steps, following the unit-2 pattern. When tokens are missing (user rows, in-flight assistant messages), I fall back to bytes4 of the text fields, estimating from the content like the user text or assistant content parts. The mass label becomes `tokens=` when provider tokens are available, or `bytes4=` as the fallback.

For the token mass of per-message rows, I'll compute the sum directly in SQL using the gauge core's SQL constants where they're exported. Looking at gauge.mjs, those constants aren't exported, so I need to check if they're available or if I should write the same SQL myself.

The SQL constants in gauge.mjs aren't being exported, so I can't access them from my plugin. I'll reuse the gauge core functions like readGauge and setDbPath for the gauge line, but I'll need to write my own SQL query for loading per-message rows since that's a different read pattern. The smoke test fixture reuses the gauge fixture pair with the session_v2 and session_message shape. I'm weighing whether to edit gauge.mjs to export those SQL constants, but that file isn't in my new-files list and the gate requires keeping the probe at 360/360 — exporting constants could risk breaking things, so I'll leave gauge.mjs alone. I'll add my own read logic in the plugin, keeping it simple by using the same DB resolution as the gauge core (the current db path via `getDbPath()`) and respecting the backend chain where it makes sense.

Now I'm checking whether the plugin should force the same backends list as the host — readGauge uses setBackends for test steering, and I need to decide if my report read should also be steerable in the smoke. The simplest approach is to use an in-process backend chain (trying node:sqlite first, then bun:sqlite) with a busy_timeout and fail-closed db-error, mirroring the gauge pattern but keeping it lighter. The DoD 2 spec says "reuse readGauge/its helpers where possible," so I'll use readGauge for the gauge line itself and write a separate `loadWindow` function for the per-message rows.

I'm weighing whether to try the spawn backend as a last resort — it would require marker SQL for rows, which adds complexity and risks buffer overflow on large databases. The spawn backend exists because bun:sqlite availability is unverified on the opencode host, but actually the bun-compiled opencode.exe host should have bun:sqlite available. I could include the spawn backend for report reads too, using a marker-row SQL pattern similar to the gauge's approach.

I'm going to reuse the gauge core's backend selection logic — I can import the gauge module and call its exported functions like setDbPath and getBackends, then replicate the three-backend logic in my plugin. This is getting complicated though, so let me stick with a pragmatic approach that mirrors the v1 design: build a minimal backend chain just for the report reads in the plugin.

For each backend, I'll try node:sqlite with a busy_timeout, then bun:sqlite with readonly mode, and finally spawn sqlite3 with one exec call using a marker row per message row (the data JSON rides in the row, escaped with a trailing marker to handle multi-line JSON). The chain order follows the gauge core's getBackends() so a smoke test forcing spawn-sqlite3 will force my reads the same way. If all backends fail, the report gets a `db-error: <deepest backend + reason>` line, matching v1's fail-closed behavior.

For the tail store and hook, the boundary lives in `.opencode/temp/context_trim_v2.json` mapping session IDs to their boundary message IDs — a JSON read-modify-write, fail-open on read, atomic-ish write. The tail command validates against the current window, and the hook handler runs on every primary request, reading the boundary from the store per session.

Now I'm thinking through how to detect and handle stale boundaries: if a boundary exists in the store but isn't strictly inside the current window (after the last completed compaction row), I need to log a `boundary-stale` line once and keep the whole window. The challenge is that the hook fires per request, so I need to cache the stale decision per session+boundary+window-head combination so the log line only appears once.

But I'm also realizing that detecting the boundary's position relative to the last compaction row requires a DB read in the hook itself—specifically, reading the session_message rows to find the last compaction row id and the boundary id. I could use a targeted SQL approach that counts window messages, checks whether the boundary exists, and determines if the boundary is before or after the last completed compaction, all without fetching the full row data. If the boundary falls strictly after the last compaction row, I'd apply the filter by dropping messages before it from the event.

messages array. For the case where there's no compaction row at all, the window becomes the full history, so a boundary in that full history is valid and I'd drop everything before it. I'm also thinking about what happens when the boundary isn't found in the DB rows at all—like when an id comes from a fork or isn't in the session—and whether that should be treated as stale or ignored silently, following the fail-open discipline.

Now I'm working through the semantics of the tail-set success line: it should show the old boundary transitioning to the new one, with the keep count, and I need to figure out what `keep` means in v2—likely the retained message count from the boundary forward. For a clear operation, passing an empty string for target would remove the boundary and return `tail-set= <old> -> none`. I also need to enforce mutual exclusion: if both target and keepMessages are provided, that's an error; if neither is provided, that's also invalid. The v1 tool had these constraints baked in, so I need to check what error messages it uses. Then in the hook, after filtering, I should verify the retained count is at least 6 on the draft side as a safety check.

The hook reads the persisted rows from the DB, but the draft messages array is the model's re-projected view—not necessarily 1:1 with DB rows, especially with assistant content projection. So I can't count draft entries against the 6-message floor. Instead, the floor validation happens at the tail-set write time, and the hook itself is a best-effort assembly filter that applies the boundary position to drop matching entries, likely by matching the draft entry's id if it carries one.

Now I need to check the SessionContext hook event shape and what fields are available in the draft messages entries—specifically whether each entry has an id that maps to the DB row id. The boundary filter drops entries whose id matches a pre-boundary row id from the DB. The tricky part is handling the window head — after a boundary is set, the draft messages still include the compaction row at the window start (if a compaction exists), and the boundary must sit strictly after the window head. If there's no compaction row, the window is all rows and the boundary is any row index i > 0 such that the remaining rows count is at least 6.

For validation, I'm keeping the v1 semantics where retained tail >= 6, which in v2 means the number of rows from the boundary onward is at least 6. The v1 keepMessages semantics count messages retained when tail_start_id is set to the target, excluding the compaction row itself. In v2, the boundary row IS included in the retained window, so I'll validate that the total rows from the boundary through the newest (inclusive) >= 6. I'll also keep the keepMessages form where N specifies the retained count — in v2 that's the row index at length - N.

For the v2 keep form, I need to define it clearly as the N-th row counting backwards from the newest, and I'll compute the boundary ID accordingly. I'll reject keep-invalid (not an integer or ≤ 0), and handle keep-exceeds-history when N is greater than or equal to the total rows — in v1 this was N > pre-compaction message count, but in v2 I'll define it as N exceeding the window size or when the computed index falls outside the valid range.

If a boundary row isn't in the session rows, I log one boundary-stale line per session-id change and keep the whole window.

For staleness detection, if the boundary's sequence is less than the last completed compaction's sequence, I drop the boundary from the store, log one `boundary-stale` line, and keep the whole post-compaction window. The stale detection works by checking where the boundary sits relative to the window head — either it's not found in the rows, or its sequence is less than the window head's sequence (meaning a new compaction row landed after it). Either case triggers the same boundary-stale line and fail-open behavior.

For REPORT, I'm structuring the header as `session <sid>` followed by `header: compaction=<id|none> boundary=<id|none> window=<N> rows=<N>` in v1 style. The v2 window is the session_message rows after the last completed compaction row (exclusive—the compaction row itself isn't sent, just what comes after it). So the header should reflect that the window size is everything after the last completed compaction, and if there's no compaction row at all, the full history is the window. I'm considering whether the boundary validation needs to account for a completed compaction, and how the v1 requirement for a "proper completed-compaction window" translates to v2's simpler model where the window head is just the row right after the last completed compaction.

The keep-floor check in v2 becomes: if the target is at window index i (0-based), then retained equals the window size minus i. I'm checking the rejection messages against v1's causes to make sure they align — v1 had `session-not-found`, `no-completed-compaction`, `target-not-found`, `target-not-before-compaction`, `retained-tail-below-floor`, `keep-invalid`, and `keep-exceeds-history`.

In v2, the boundary sits strictly inside the window after the last compaction row, with a retained tail of at least 6 messages. If the target matches the window's first row, that's allowed since v1 permitted index 0. If it lands on or before the compaction row, I reject it with `target-not-inside-window`. I'm debating whether v2 requires the compaction to be "completed" or just "durable" — the history loader filters for completed status, so I'll stick with completed only. If there's no compaction row, the whole history is the window and a boundary at any index is valid as long as it keeps at least 6 messages.

And whether the domain method takes `{sessionID}` directly (input: SessionContextInput = { sessionID: string }).

Note: this method gives the CURRENT WINDOW rows only (after last compaction), including the compaction row itself (history.load uses `gte(compaction.seq)` — the compaction row IS the first row of the window, per the `previousCompaction` note: "History loads from the latest completed compaction, so a previous one is always the first message."). Wait — `latestCompaction` returns the seq of the latest completed compaction and then rows with `seq >= compaction.seq` — INCLUDING the compaction row itself. So the v2 window INCLUDES the compaction row as the first entry. In v1, the marker row is NOT in the model window (the [marker, summary] pair is excluded from the rows). In v2, the compaction row is INCLUDED as row 1 (it carries the summary text + recent).

Trim = drop everything before the boundary → the LATER the boundary, the MORE dropped. v1's "high" = index 0 = the LEAST dropped (largest retained). Confusing: v1's "high retention" = keep most. In v2: high (retention) = the earliest legal anchor a_min (keeps the most rows); low = the latest legal anchor = keep = FLOOR (the most aggressive trim): index N - FLOOR. So:
- high = a_min (max retention);
- low = N - FLOOR (the floor = the most aggressive trim allowed);
- medium = midpoint(a_min, N - FLOOR).
And the current boundary is reported in the header, plus a marker on the matching option if it coincides. v1's rule "low = status quo" is replaced (documented deviation: in v2 the block lists the trim RANGE from the floor (low = the most aggressive legal trim) to the max retention (high), medium = midpoint; the CURRENT boundary is in the header line). Cleaner and honest. Emit only when N - a_min >= FLOOR (a trim range exists).

keep counts per option = N - idx (retained rows).

Biggest-droppable: heaviest finished turns (assistant rows with time.completed and no error) in the window → `biggest: <row#>=<mass> ...` top-5. Note: only rows AFTER the head can be dropped; the head row itself isn't droppable, so exclude index 0 from the candidate list (documented).

Dry-run: target index i (must be a row id). remove = count of rows strictly before i and after the head... v1: remove = targetIndex - oldStart (oldStart = the current tail head) — i.e., what ADDITIONAL rows the new boundary drops vs the current boundary. Let me mirror: oldStart = current effective start (boundary index if valid else 0... hmm in v1 oldStart = w.tailIndex (current tail) or compactionIndex when degenerate). In v2: oldEffective = (valid stored boundary index) else 1 if headIsCompaction else 0. remove = max(0, i - oldEffective)... hmm careful: a boundary EARLIER than the current one INCREASES retention (negative remove). v1 printed remove only when targetIndex > oldStart, else 0. Keep that: remove = max(0, i - oldEffective), removeMass = sum of rows [oldEffective, i) when i > oldEffective else 0; keep = N - i, keepMass = sum rows[i..N). verdict = the tail validation for that target (ok / rejected: cause).

Also the report must reflect the CURRENT window state header:
`header: head=<id> head-type=<compaction|message> rows=<N> boundary=<id> boundary-index=<i> retained=<N-i>` — with boundary=none when absent/stale. And when the stored boundary is NOT in the window → `boundary=<id> boundary=stale`? Do it as `boundary=<id-or-none>` plus `boundary-state=inside|stale|absent`. Let me finalize the header line:
`header: head=<id|none> head-type=<compaction|row|empty> rows=<N> boundary=<id|none> boundary-state=<inside|stale|absent> retained=<N-i|N>`
